> They said: "We are under no obligation towards the unlettered (gentiles)." And they speak falsehood about God while they know. (75) Not so! Whoever fulfils his covenant and is God-fearing, surely God loves the God-fearing. (76) (30)

> And the Jews and the Christians said: "We are the sons of God and His beloved." Say: "Then why does He punish you for your sins? No, you are human beings among those He created. He forgives whom He wills and punishes whom He wills, and to God belongs the kingdom of the heavens and the earth and what lies between them, and to Him is the final return." (31)

Then God shows them the true measure of distinction and merit, saying:

> Say: "O People of the Book, you stand on nothing until you uphold the Torah and the Gospel and what has been sent down to you from your Lord." (32)

This false belief in their racial and religious distinction, and that they are God's chosen people, has deposited in their souls a kind of deviation and a feeling that they are a special kind of human being, superior to other peoples in intelligence, wisdom and everything else, and that they alone have the right to dominion over all mankind.

(29) Al-Baqara 2:135-136.

(30) Al Imran 3:75-76.

(31) Al-Ma'ida 5:18.

(32) Al-Ma'ida 5:68.

{53}------------------------------------------------

This false belief led them to regard the rest of the peoples as unbelievers who deserve life only to serve them, and to regard their hatred and loathing of these peoples as a means of drawing near to God. Likewise, their belief that their religion is peculiar to them, a favour by which God preferred them over mankind as His only people whom He chose for Himself from among the peoples, led them to isolation, haughtiness and closing themselves off, and to blocking every path of cooperation and brotherhood with others. It raised a thick wall of hatred and intense rancour between them and the various peoples: an isolation founded on hatred and aggression, not on peaceableness, the isolation of one lying in wait for the chance to pounce on his enemy.

This rancour, this wall of hatred founded on haughtiness and on religious and racial chauvinism, offers sufficient explanation for the disturbance of the Jewish psyche, its intense tension and its constant feeling of anxiety and alienation. Its effect was reflected not only in their relations with other peoples but also in their internal relations among themselves, and what their own books relate confirms the truth of what we say. They are always, as the Noble Qur'an says:

> Their hostility among themselves is severe. You think them united, but their hearts are divided. (33)

Spinoza, the Jewish philosopher, gives us a precise analysis that helps us see the effect of the racial tendency on the psychological and moral make-up of the Jews. He says: "The joy a man feels from believing himself superior to others, if it is not a childish feeling, arises only from envy or from a rancorous heart. For example, true well-being and the happiness of man lie in wisdom alone and in the knowledge of what is true, and not at all in being wiser than others, or in others being deprived of wisdom; for that will never increase his own pure wisdom or his true well-being. Whoever rejoices in that rejoices in the misfortune of others, and is envious and wicked, knowing neither true wisdom nor true peace of soul." (34)

Dr. Fouad Zakariya likewise analyses the kind of fanaticism peculiar to the Jews and its effect in shaping their psyche and their relations with others. Having defined negative fanaticism

(33) Al-Hashr 59:14.

(34) Risala fi al-Lahut wa al-Siyasa (Theological-Political Treatise), p. 171, translated by Hasan Hanafi, Egyptian General Authority for Authorship and Publishing, 1971.

{54}------------------------------------------------

as "at bottom a negative view of others; the fanatic's thinking is directed towards causing harm to others more than it inclines to asserting his own merits or gaining some benefit for himself" (35), he says:

"It is common, in analysing the structure of fanaticism, to say that fanaticism arises first in the majority against the minority, and that the fanaticism of the latter is merely a defensive reaction by which it protects itself from the persecution that the majority inflicts on it ... But there are a few cases which dialectical analysis shows to depart from this familiar pattern, that is, cases in which fanaticism begins in the minority, and the majority is forced into defensive reactions against it, or into practising a counter-fanaticism harsher and more violent than the original. Our present age has seen a unique example of this kind of fanaticism in Rhodesia and South Africa ...

"The history of Judaism, however, may be counted a glaring example, stretching over many long centuries, of this unique kind of minority fanaticism against the majority. It is worth mentioning that the Jewish minority was never in any case a ruling minority in control of the state, as is the case with the Europeans in Rhodesia and South Africa; it was a politically weak minority. Even so, at the very depth of its weakness it practised a kind of provocation that drove the society in which it lived to persecute it against that society's will.

"For the myth of God's chosen people, whatever may be said of it, plays a real role in the Jewish heritage. It is true that the enlightened among the heirs of this heritage try to interpret it in non-racial senses, but there is conclusive evidence that this myth forms an inseparable part of the psychological make-up of the ordinary Jew, and drives him to kinds of behaviour that must in the end lead to a clash between him and his society ...

"Perhaps the most telling proof of what we say is that the Jews, however weak they may be in a given society, refuse to integrate into that society and regard such integration as a sign of their collapse, and so they work to resist this collapse with all the strength they have. Yet integration into the majority, and obtaining the same rights on an equal footing, is the distant dream

(35) Ara' Naqdiyya fi Mushkilat al-Fikr wa al-Thaqafa (Critical Views on Problems of Thought and Culture), p. 50, Egyptian General Book Authority, 1975.

{55}------------------------------------------------

for which persecuted minorities struggle in every part of the world ... In the case of the Jewish minority, however, integration is regarded as the most heinous crime it could commit against itself: it is a betrayal of the Jewish heritage and the loss of everything that distinguishes [the chosen people]. Hence the difficulty of dealing with Jewish minorities, and the inevitability that such dealing turns into persecution even in societies that had no original intention of practising it. For if these societies granted the Jewish minority equal rights and worked to integrate it and dissolve it completely, this integration would be met with violent resistance from it; and if they left it to live on the margin of the larger community, its cries of complaint about persecution would rise.

"In truth, a certain feeling of injustice and persecution was, and still is, an inseparable part of the driving force that helped the Jews hold together and preserve their heritage through the ages. If we grasp this fact well, it becomes clear to us that reaching a final settlement on the basis of peaceful coexistence with a state founded on a racial basis, such as Israel, is something that comes close to the impossible." (36)

It is enough for us here to present some of the episodes of the Jews as the Qur'an mentions them, as a model from which the characteristics of the Jewish psyche and character become clear. We have taken care that these episodes agree with what their own books mention, which are in fact more expansive in drawing a dark and frightening picture of the character and nature of the Jews.

Pharaoh had been killing their sons and sparing their women, and inflicting on them the worst torment, abasement and humiliation. When God saved them from all of this, and before their sandals had dried from the wet floor of the sea that God had parted to save them, they wished that Moses would make them an idol to worship:

> And We brought the Children of Israel across the sea, and they came upon a people devoted to idols of theirs. They said: "O Moses, make for us a god just as they have gods." He said: "You are indeed an ignorant people. (138) As for these, what they are engaged in is doomed to destruction, and vain is what they have been doing." (139) He said: "Shall I seek for you a god other than God

{56}------------------------------------------------

> ...a god, when He has favoured you above all the worlds?" And [recall] when We delivered you from the people of Pharaoh, who inflicted on you the worst of torment, slaying your sons and sparing your women; and in that was a mighty trial from your Lord. (37)

God accepted their repentance for the sin of their hearts and the corruption of their wishes. But hardly had Moses (peace be upon him) left them to receive the revelation that would guide them and bring them happiness than they passed from wishing to doing. They fashioned a calf with their own hands and fell down prostrate before it:

> And the people of Moses, after him, took from their ornaments a calf, a body that lowed. Did they not see that it did not speak to them nor guide them to any path? They took it [for worship], and they were wrongdoers. And when they were struck with remorse and saw that they had gone astray, they said: "If our Lord does not have mercy on us and forgive us, we shall surely be among the losers." (38)

No sooner were they enjoying God's mercy than their doubts and their materialist nature overcame them, and they said to Moses: we will not believe you until we see [God] with our own eyes:

> And [recall] when you said: "O Moses, we will not believe you until we see God plainly," and the thunderbolt seized you while you looked on. Then We raised you up after your death, that you might give thanks. (39)

(37) Al-A'raf 138-141.

(38) Al-A'raf 148-149.

(39) Al-Baqara: 55-56.

{57}------------------------------------------------

But they did not give thanks. Indeed, they could not even bear the freedom God had granted them by delivering them from Pharaoh's bondage, since it came with a little hardship, and they cried out in the face of Moses and Aaron: "Would that we had died by the hand of the Lord in the land of Egypt, when we sat by the pots of meat and ate bread to the full! For you have brought us out into this wilderness" (40).

God had mercy on their weakness: He made springs gush forth for them from the rocks and sent down upon them manna and quails:

> And We revealed to Moses, when his people asked him for water: "Strike the rock with your staff." And there gushed forth from it twelve springs; each people knew its drinking place. And We shaded them with clouds and sent down upon them manna and quails: "Eat of the good things We have provided for you." And they did not wrong Us, but it was themselves they wronged. (160) (41)

Yes, they wronged themselves. Just as they could not endure affliction, they could not endure blessing. When they had eaten and drunk their fill, insolence filled their souls, and they sought to exchange what is better for what is lower:

> And [recall] when you said: "O Moses, we cannot endure one kind of food, so call upon your Lord for us to bring forth for us of what the earth grows: its herbs, its cucumbers, its garlic, its lentils and its onions." He said: "Would you exchange what is lower for what is better? Go down to any town (*misr*), and you shall have what you have asked for." And humiliation and wretchedness were stamped upon them, and they incurred wrath from

(40) Exodus 16: 2-3.

(41) Al-A'raf: 160.

{58}------------------------------------------------

> God. That was because they used to disbelieve in the signs of God and slay the prophets without right. That was because they disobeyed and used to transgress. (42)

The humiliation and wretchedness that possessed their hearts were stamped upon them at the gates of the land (*misr*) that God had promised them, in which was all they desired. No sooner had they seen its inhabitants than they said to Moses: go, you and your Lord, and fight; we shall sit here:

> And [recall] when Moses said to his people: "O my people, remember God's favour upon you when He placed prophets among you and made you kings, and gave you what He had not given anyone among the worlds. O my people, enter the Holy Land which God has ordained for you, and do not turn back on your heels, lest you turn back as losers." They said: "O Moses, in it is a people of tyrants, and we shall not enter it until they leave it; if they leave it, then we shall enter." Two men among those who feared [God], upon whom God had bestowed His favour, said: "Enter upon them through the gate; once you have entered it, you will be victorious. And put your trust in God, if you are believers." They said: "O Moses, we shall never enter it as long as they are in it. So go, you and your Lord, and fight; we shall sit right here." He said: "My Lord, I have power over none but myself and my brother, so separate us from the rebellious people." He said: "Then it shall be forbidden to them for forty years; they shall wander in the land. So do not grieve over

{59}------------------------------------------------

> the rebellious people." (43)

At last they entered Palestine, though faith had not yet entered their hearts. Blessing only increased them in insolence and corruption, and there befell them, at the hands of the Philistines, the same humiliation and degradation that had befallen them at the hands of the Egyptians. When their own souls grew narrow upon them through the evil they had earned, and the earth grew narrow upon them through the wickedness they had wrought in it, and their end came into view, they ran in alarm to a prophet of theirs, asking him to set over them one who would rescue them from the depths of vice and degradation, and they pledged to God obedience and loyalty. But when God chose the rescuer for them, their souls could not refrain from disputation and obstinacy, and they said: this is no king; he is not the greatest of us in wealth or standing. When their prophet showed the folly of their materialist measures of things, they would not accept until he brought them a sign from God that their eyes could witness and by which their hearts could be reassured. And when God supported the one He had chosen as their king with what they had asked for, they marched with him unwillingly, and at the very first test of their resolve and sincerity in fighting they fell away, all but the few who believed and were true to what they had pledged to God:

> Have you not considered the chiefs of the Children of Israel after Moses, when they said to a prophet of theirs: "Raise up for us a king, and we shall fight in the way of God"? He said: "Might it be that, if fighting were prescribed for you, you would not fight?" They said: "And why should we not fight in the way of God, when we have been driven from our homes and our children?" But when fighting was prescribed for them, they turned away, all but a few of them; and God knows the wrongdoers. (246) And their prophet said to them: "God has raised up Talut (Saul) for you as king." They said: "How can he have kingship over us, when we are more entitled to kingship than he, and he has not been given abundance of wealth?" He said: "God has chosen him over you and has increased him abundantly in knowledge and in stature. And God gives His kingship

(43) Al-Ma'ida: 20-26; and see the Book of Numbers 13, 14.

{60}------------------------------------------------

> to whom He wills; and God is All-Encompassing, All-Knowing." (247) And their prophet said to them: "The sign of his kingship is that the Ark will come to you, in which is tranquillity from your Lord and a remnant of what the family of Moses and the family of Aaron left behind, borne by the angels. In that is a sign for you, if you are believers." (248) And when Talut set out with the troops, he said: "God will test you with a river. Whoever drinks from it is not of me, and whoever does not taste it is of me, except one who scoops up a handful with his hand." But they drank from it, all but a few of them. And when he had crossed it, he and those who believed with him, they said: "We have no strength today against Jalut (Goliath) and his troops." Those who were certain that they would meet God said: "How many a small band has overcome a large band by God's leave! And God is with the steadfast." (249) (44)

These are some of the pictures the Qur'an has drawn of the morals and beliefs of the Jews. After them we present a final picture, drawn by the Talmud, which in their eyes is holier than the Torah, and in which the psychological, moral and doctrinal dimension of the Jewish character becomes clear. The Talmud says:

> "The day is twelve hours. In the first three of them God sits and studies the Law; in the second three He judges; in the third three He feeds the world; and in the last three He sits and plays with the whale.
>
> God repents of having left the Jews in a state of misery, so much so that He strikes His face and weeps every day, and two tears fall from His eyes into the sea, whose roar is heard from the beginning of the world to its end. The waters are troubled and the earth trembles most of the time, and so earthquakes occur.

(44) Al-Baqara: 246-249.

{61}------------------------------------------------

> God created the devils on Friday when darkness fell, and created for them neither bodies nor clothing, because the Sabbath was near and He did not have enough time to make all that.
>
> The souls of the Jews are distinguished from all other souls in being part of God, as the son is part of his father; hence the souls of the Jews are dearer to God than all other souls. None enters Paradise but the Jews. As for the Fire, it is the abode of the unbelievers, and they have no portion in it but weeping, because of the darkness, stench and mud in it.
>
> The difference between the rank of man and that of the animal is like the difference between the Jews and the other peoples. The world and all that is in it belongs to the Jews; they have the right to dominate it and absolute disposal over everything.
>
> It is permitted to cheat a non-Jew and take his money by means of exorbitant usury; but if you sell anything to your Jewish brother or buy anything from him, do not deceive him or cheat him.
>
> God does not forgive the sin of a Jew who returns to a gentile his lost property, and it is not permissible to return things lost by foreigners. A Jew is not permitted to lend to a foreigner except at usury.
>
> A Jew does no wrong if he violates the honour of a foreign woman, for every woman who is not of the Children of Israel is a beast. Everyone who is not a Jew is a foreigner. Every marriage contract of non-Jews is void. If a Jew commits adultery with a Christian woman, he has not committed anything forbidden; indeed the Jews have the right to rape non-Jewish women. A Jewish wife may make no complaint if her husband commits adultery with a foreign woman in the dwelling where he lives with his wife.
>
> A Jew must swear twenty false oaths rather than expose any of his Jewish brethren to any harm.
>
> The Jews must treat the Christians as base, irrational animals.
>
> Those outside the religion of the Jews are unclean swine. God created the foreigner in human form so that he might be fit to serve the Jews, for whose sake the world was created.
>
> It is not just for a Jew to pity his enemies and show them mercy.
>
> A Jew is not permitted to greet an unbeliever with peace unless he fears harm or enmity from him. Hypocrisy is permitted, and there is no harm in feigning love for the unbeliever if the

{62}------------------------------------------------

> Jew fears harm from him (45)."

These, then, are the morals of the Jews. They rest on one fundamental foundation: excessive materialism and self-love. This is the basis of all the vices that have marked their morals, whether towards their prophets, in themselves, or towards other peoples.

- Towards their prophets: injury and killing, doubt and disobedience, an inclination to idolatry and unbelief, and the alteration (*tahrif*) of God's law.

- In themselves: cowardice, anxiety, hesitation, a sense of alienation and fear of others.

- Towards other peoples: rancour and hatred, unjust arrogance, lying in wait, and an unwillingness to live in peace.

---

(45) Siyasat al-Isti'mar wa al-Sahyuniyya (The Policy of Colonialism and Zionism), p. 7, note 5, Hasan Sabri al-Khuli, Dar al-Ma'arif, Cairo, 1973.

{63}------------------------------------------------

## The Alteration of Their Holy Books:

In truth, this is a question on which debate has ended among trustworthy scholars and researchers. There is no longer any disagreement worth mentioning among them about the Jews' alteration of their holy books; the differences between them lie only in the quantity and kind of evidence they offer. The evidence we present here is nothing more than an addition to, or a reminder of, the evidence they have established:

### 1. The Swelling of the Torah at the Hands of the Jews:

Here we shall rely on the Torah itself. The Torah we have in our hands today cannot possibly contain the same quantity of words with which it was revealed. We see its five books filling 379 pages in small type, whereas the Torah itself defines for us the form in which it was revealed, in a way that leaves no room for comparison between its present size and the size in which it was revealed. The Book of Exodus states that Moses received the Torah written on two tablets of stone:

> "And the Lord said to Moses: Come up to the mountain and be there, and I will give you the tablets of stone, and the law and the commandment which I have written to teach them" (46).

> "And Moses turned and went down from the mountain, with the two tablets of the testimony in his hand, tablets written on both their sides; on the one side and on the other they were written" (47).

It may occur to the mind that there is no rational objection to the two tablets being so large and wide that what was inscribed on them would fill 379 pages of paper, as we see it now.

But the Torah itself rules out this possibility when it defines the size of the two tablets by the ark that God commanded Moses to make and in which to keep the Torah:

(46) 24: 12.

(47) 32: 15.

{64}------------------------------------------------

> "An ark of wood, two and a half cubits long, a cubit and a half wide and a cubit and a half high; and you shall put into the ark the testimony which I shall give you" (48).

The possibility may also occur to the mind that the letters written on the two tablets were so small that, if its words were copied onto paper, they would fill many pages; we see today the Noble Qur'an written on a single page not much more than a cubit by a cubit.

Once again we find the Torah itself ruling out this possibility, when it defines for us the time its writing and reading took, so that no doubt at all remains for us in estimating its size: it amounted to no more than words that would not fill more than a few leaves.

> "And Moses wrote this law and delivered it to the priests, the sons of Levi, who bore the ark of the covenant of the Lord, and to all the elders of Israel. And Moses commanded them, saying: ... at the Feast of Tabernacles ... you shall read this law before all Israel in their hearing" (49).

The Book of Joshua defines the time more precisely still, when it relates that Joshua inscribed a copy of the Torah on stone and recited it to the people at a single sitting:

> "And he wrote there on the stones a copy of the law of Moses. And afterwards he read all the words of the law, the blessing and the curse, according to all that is written in the book of the law. There was not a word of all that Moses commanded which Joshua did not read before all the assembly of Israel" (50).

But a last possibility may arise: is it not possible that Moses (peace be upon him) received other texts along with the two tablets, and that the whole is what is called the Torah?

Yet we find this last possibility refuted by the First Book of Kings, which states clearly that when Solomon moved the ark into the temple he had built, there was nothing in it but the two tablets:

> "There was nothing in the ark except the two tablets of stone which Moses had put there at Horeb, when the Lord made a covenant with the Children of Israel when they came out of the land of Egypt" (51).

(48) Exodus 25: 10; 16.

(49) Deuteronomy: 34.

(50) Book of Joshua: 9.

(51) 8: 9.

{65}------------------------------------------------

Finally, the Torah specifies for us, on the tongue of Moses (peace be upon him) before his death, the content of the Torah: that it consists of the Ten Commandments, and that these alone are what was written on the two tablets:

> "And Moses called all Israel and said to them: Hear, O Israel, the statutes and the ordinances which I speak in your hearing this day, and learn them and take care to do them. ... He said: I am the Lord your God, who brought you out of the land of Egypt, out of the house of bondage. You shall have no other gods before me. You shall not make for yourself a graven image ... You shall not take the name of the Lord your God in vain ... You shall not kill, and you shall not commit adultery, and you shall not steal, and you shall not bear false witness against your neighbour, and you shall not covet your neighbour's wife, and you shall not covet your neighbour's house, nor his field, nor his manservant, nor his maidservant, nor his ox, nor his ass, nor anything that is your neighbour's. These words the Lord spoke to all your assembly on the mountain, out of the midst of the fire, the cloud and the thick darkness, with a loud voice, and He added no more. And He wrote them on two tablets of stone and gave them to me" (52).

All this makes it likely, if it does not confirm it, that the Torah which Moses wrote on the two tablets was no more than the Ten Commandments. This is what agrees with reason and with the texts we have cited from the Torah itself, especially when we know that *sefer* ("book") in the Hebrew language means a leaf or a sheet. As for the mass, the swelling, in which the Torah now stands, it is the work of human beings.

## 2. The Loss of the Torah, or the Break in Its Chain of Transmission:

Besides what we have mentioned of the Philistines' seizure of the holy ark, the temple that Solomon (peace be upon him) built, and in which he placed the ark of the Lord containing nothing but the two tablets, was plundered and destroyed twice after Solomon.

The first time was at the hands of the Egyptians in the reign of Rehoboam son of Solomon. The First Book of Kings [14: 25] says: "And in the fifth year of King Rehoboam, Shishak king of Egypt came up against Jerusalem. And he took away the treasures of the house of the Lord and the treasures of the king's house; he took away everything, and he took away all the shields of gold which Solomon had made."

The evidence that the Torah was lost in this destruction is that there is no mention of it until the priest Hilkiah announced, in the reign of King Josiah son of Amon, who ruled the kingdom of Judah 629-

(52) Deuteronomy 5: 1-22.

{66}------------------------------------------------

598 BC, at a time when that kingdom was breathing its last because of the unbelief and heresy into which it had fallen, that he had found the Torah. This announcement by Hilkiah means that it had been lost. Researchers doubt Hilkiah's truthfulness, because it is not reasonable that the Torah should have been in Jerusalem all that time without anyone finding it before him. They therefore hold that this Torah was composed by Hilkiah himself, who relied in writing it on the legends and stories the Israelites and their elders used to repeat. As for the Torah revealed to Moses, nothing of it remained but the Ten Commandments; what helped those commandments survive was the ease with which they could be memorised and handed down orally from generation to generation (53).

The second destruction was at the hands of Nebuchadnezzar, who destroyed the Temple completely and burned all of Jerusalem. After this destruction the Jews never rose again. This is the description of what happened in the Second Book of Chronicles 36: 11-20: "And he, that is, Nebuchadnezzar king of Babylon, made Zedekiah king over Judah and Jerusalem. Zedekiah was twenty-one years old when he became king ... And he did evil in the sight of the Lord his God, and did not humble himself before Jeremiah the prophet, who spoke from the mouth of the Lord. He also rebelled against King Nebuchadnezzar ... Moreover all the chiefs of the priests and the people multiplied their transgressions according to all the abominations of the nations, and they polluted the house of the Lord which He had hallowed in Jerusalem ... But they mocked the messengers of God, despised His words and scoffed at His prophets, until the wrath of the Lord arose ... Therefore He brought up against them the king of the Chaldeans, who slew their young men with the sword in the house of their sanctuary, and had no compassion on young man or virgin, old man or aged; He gave them all into his hand. And all the vessels of the house of God, great and small, and the treasures of the house of the Lord, and the treasures of the king and of his princes, all these he brought to Babylon. And they burned the house of God and broke down the wall of Jerusalem and burned all its palaces with fire ... and they became servants to him and to his sons."

### 3. The Old Testament Is the Rabbis' Selection:

The books that the Old Testament contains in their present form were selected by the rabbis after the Babylonian captivity from among an enormous number of conflicting books, all of which were attributed to Moses and the prophets of the Children of Israel. After the destruction of their state in Palestine, and the dispersal of those of them who survived Nebuchadnezzar's onslaught to scattered regions, the rabbis and leaders of the Jews feared that the Israelites would be lost as a people and dissolve into the peoples among whom they had taken refuge. So they convened

(53) Muqaranat al-Adyan: al-Yahudiyya (Comparative Religion: Judaism), p. 112 ff.

{67}------------------------------------------------

a council of theirs in Alexandria in 130 BC, which collected what religious heritage was available to it and codified it without scrutiny, then translated it into Greek, in order to preserve the history and unity of the Jews. But the Sadducees and the Samaritans recognised nothing of what the council approved except the five books attributed to Moses. Then another council was held in Palestine around AD 95 to review and refine the heritage collected by the council of Alexandria. But that council split: the Pharisees rejected any text in the Greek language and regarded as holy only the books written in Hebrew, while the rabbis of Alexandria accepted many books the Pharisees had not accepted and added them to the holy books. Thus the Jews came to have two different versions of the Bible, quite apart from the fact that many Jews recognised none of this except the five books of Moses. Moreover, the rabbis who authorised the holy books were not above suspicion and were not competent for so grave a task. Among them, as Spinoza says, were "unbelievers and profligates who attained the office of high priest by criminal means, and who nonetheless had, according to the commandments of the Book, absolute authority in interpreting the Law" (54). This testimony of Spinoza is supported by what we have set out about the morals of the Jews.

#### 4. Objective Criticism of the Text

If we look at the texts of the Torah themselves, it becomes clear to us beyond any room for doubt that they are of human composition, or that the word of revelation has been mixed in them with the word of man. Any reader will realise at first glance that they could only have been dictated by a human spirit and could not be of divine dictation, or at the very least that they have suffered such severe alteration as to disqualify them from being a holy document relied upon as revelation sent down from God.

Here are some observations that make this statement clear:

- The Torah not only speaks of Moses in the third person; it also gives numerous testimonies about him, such as: "Moses wrote this law; God spoke with Moses; God was with Moses face to face; Moses was a very meek man, more than all men; Moses was angry with the officers of the army; Moses the man of God; so Moses the servant of the Lord died in the land of Moab, and no

(54) Risala fi al-Siyasa wa al-Lahut (A Treatise on Politics and Theology), p. 262; and see note 2, p. 35.

{68}------------------------------------------------

man knows his grave to this day; and there has not arisen since a prophet in Israel like Moses ..." (55).

It is clear from this style that Moses did not write these words; rather they were written by someone else who came long after him, perhaps as late as the age of Jesus (peace be upon him). For the judgment that no prophet like Moses has since arisen among the Children of Israel can only be made by someone who surveys the history of the Children of Israel to its end, especially as he says of Moses: and no one knows his grave to this day. One who lived close to the time of Moses' death would not say such a thing.

- Extreme exaggeration that contradicts the laws of nature. For example, Genesis 46: 26 says: "All the souls of the sons of Jacob who came into Egypt were seventy," whereas the Book of Numbers states that the number of the Israelites immediately after they left Egypt was more than five million souls. Chapter 1: 44-48 says: "So all those numbered of the Children of Israel, by their fathers' houses, from twenty years old and upward ... all those numbered were six hundred and three thousand five hundred and fifty. But the Levites by the tribe of their fathers were not numbered among them." And "all those numbered of the Levites, whom Moses and Aaron numbered at the command of the Lord, by their families, every male from a month old and upward, were twenty-two thousand" (56).

This means that the number of the Israelites grew in two centuries from seventy individuals to more than five million souls; that is, their number multiplied more than seventy thousand times in a period of no more than two centuries. This is inconceivable and does not accord with the laws of nature, when we know that the number of Jews today, after nearly three thousand years have passed since their exodus from Egypt, has not reached double that figure.

- It is enough to set out a simple comparison between the Noble Qur'an and the books of the Jews where they speak of the prophets and messengers, to grasp the vast difference between the language of

(55) Book of Deuteronomy 13, 31, 34; and Book of Numbers 12, 13.

(56) Book of Numbers 3: 39.

{69}------------------------------------------------

revelation and the language of men, which contradicts every divine value and ideal.

For example, whereas the Noble Qur'an says of Aaron (peace be upon him):

> And We indeed conferred Our favour upon Moses and Aaron, and We delivered them and their people from the great distress, and We helped them, so that it was they who prevailed. And We gave them the clarifying Book, and guided them to the straight path, and left for them [a good name] among later generations: "Peace be upon Moses and Aaron!" Thus do We reward those who do good. They were truly among Our believing servants. (57)

we find that the Torah, which acknowledges Aaron as a prophet, makes of him an idolater who fashions idols for them with his own hands and joins them in worshipping them, as mentioned above. What contradiction could be greater than this?!

And whereas the Noble Qur'an says of David (peace be upon him):

> And remember Our servant David, the man of strength; he was ever turning [to God]. We made the mountains glorify [God] with him at evening and at sunrise, and the birds, gathered together, all turning to Him. And We strengthened his kingdom and gave him wisdom and decisive speech. (58)

their books, which acknowledge him as a prophet, make him a man of base soul and a profligate. The Second Book of Samuel 11: 2 says: "But David remained in Jerusalem. And it happened at evening time that David arose from his bed and walked on the roof of the king's

(57) Al-Saffat: 114-122.

(58) Sad: 17-20.

{70}------------------------------------------------

house, and from the roof he saw a woman bathing, and the woman was very beautiful to look upon ... And David sent messengers and took her, and she came in to him, and he lay with her ... and she conceived by him." The book then relates how David plunged deeper into sin and set about killing the woman's husband treacherously and perfidiously in order to add her to his harem, and that she then bore him Solomon (peace be upon him).

As for Solomon (peace be upon him), of whom the Qur'an says:

> And to David We gave Solomon. How excellent a servant! He was ever turning [to God]. (59)

we find their books making of him a profligate and an unbeliever with no concern but to sink himself in lusts, and making of his house a den of debauchery, vice and unbelief. The First Book of Kings 11: 1-6 says: "And Solomon loved many foreign women, together with the daughter of Pharaoh, from the nations of which the Lord had said to the Children of Israel: You shall not go in to them, nor shall they come in to you, for they will turn your hearts after their gods. Solomon clung to these in love. And he had seven hundred wives, princesses, and three hundred concubines, and his wives turned his heart after other gods, and his heart was not wholly with the Lord his God. So Solomon went after Ashtoreth, the goddess of the Sidonians, and Solomon did evil in the sight of the Lord, and did not follow the Lord fully." You may see for yourselves how far alteration has afflicted the holy books of the Jews.

### The Testimony of Spinoza:

Finally, many Jewish, Christian and Muslim historians are now certain that the Torah we have in our hands today is not the Torah of Moses but was composed by the Jews themselves many centuries after the death of Moses (peace be upon him). Their efforts now turn on determining who composed this Torah and when it was written. Among them it is enough for us to take the testimony of the Jewish philosopher Spinoza, known for his learning and his religiosity, who on that account cannot be accused of ignorance or bigotry. After presenting many proofs and observations on the alteration of the Torah, Spinoza says: "From all these observations it appears as clear as daylight that Moses

(59) Sad: 30.

{71}------------------------------------------------

did not write the five books; rather they were written by someone who lived many centuries after Moses" (60).

Spinoza then goes on to criticise the remaining books and concludes that all the books contained in the Old Testament were written by a single historian who wished to relate the ancient history of the Jews from their first beginnings until the destruction of Jerusalem by Nebuchadnezzar. Whoever leafs through these books from first to last will see that they bear three principal characteristics:

- Unity of purpose and style in all the books.
- The connection between them in one and the same manner.
- Their composition many centuries after the events narrated.
- All this confirms that a single historian wrote them.

As for who this author or writer might be, Spinoza considers it most likely that he was Ezra, who was known for his devotion to the study of the Law and his complete mastery of the religious heritage of the Jews, and who took charge of their affairs by mandate from Cyrus, king of the Persians, after the Babylonian captivity (61).

I believe that after the testimony of Spinoza the Jew, and the evidence we have presented, which is little compared with what historians and researchers in this field have presented, no doubt remains for us that the holy books of the Jews have been altered completely. Even if something of the words of revelation has remained in them, it cannot be distinguished with certainty, and so reliance on them as religious documents of any standing falls away. That is to say, it is not possible, relying on reason, to distinguish the milla in them from the nihla, or revelation from opinion, except by relying on another source, namely the Qur'an.

## Its Origin and Development

Discussion of Christianity<sup>(1)</sup> cannot be separated, as regards its foundations and sources, from discussion of Judaism, because of the close historical and religious bond between them. Although the Jews do not recognise Christianity, whether its book, its prophethood, its doctrines or its rites, Christianity by its books and its messengers has a lineage going back to the Children of Israel and their books. This means that discussion of Christianity is merely an extension of discussion of Judaism, and our criticism of the Torah, in its text and its chain of transmission, necessarily extends its effect to Christianity as regards *milla* (religion as God revealed it) and *nihla* (a claim or doctrine put forward by human beings), since Christianity makes the Old Testament part of its wealth, the soil into which its roots reach, and the reference on which it relies for its doctrines, symbols and rituals. And yet Christianity as it is now, though it officially acknowledges the paternity of the Torah, is in reality an embryo, if we may use the expression, formed from many waters, which passed through its stages in several wombs, Jerusalem, Alexandria, Tarsus, Antioch and Athens, until its formation was completed and it was born in Rome, having become another creature altogether. Of the East it retains only veins connected to it that supply it with its blood, its thought and its doctrines, which ensure its survival and life; the West was a womb in which it was nurtured and which supplied it with nourishment that shaped its structure and completed its stages. Indeed, we would not stray from the truth if we said that Christianity, in its origin and development, was a strange creature, unique among living beings in having more than one father and more than one womb. It belongs neither to the East alone nor to the West alone, but to both together, such that if we removed the veins of life linking it to the East or to the West, nothing of it would remain, and it would vanish like a creature of dust, if we may use the expression. For despite its swelling and bloating, it does not carry within itself the vital organs that would ensure its survival and continuity. It is a shadow, not a reality; a mirror, not a fact; a compound without elements; a mixture of illusions and fancies without form or bounds.

(1) We recall that the religion and message of Jesus (peace be upon him) is Islam. That name [Christianity] is a man-made, later designation whose use became widespread after the message of Jesus was transformed into a new and different religion in about the fourth century. See on this Charles Guignebert, Al-Masihiyya: Nash'atuha wa Tatawwuruha (Christianity: Its Origin and Development), Chapter Seven.

{76}------------------------------------------------

If we look at its oldest documents, we find them distinguished from all other texts by the weakness of their chain of transmission, by confusion and by the difficulty of verifying them. Nothing shows this better than the following: "The careful research conducted in recent years on the basis of the original documents has established only a portrayal of the life of Jesus with some degree of certainty and confirmation, and we must regard the books that claim to recount his life as works resting on a great deal of arbitrariness and subjective tendency"<sup>(2)</sup>. "Merely leafing through the Gospels is enough to convince us that their authors arrived at plainly contradictory constructions of the same events and sayings, which compels us to say that they did not seek factual truth in writing them and did not draw on a fixed history imposing the sequence of events upon them; on the contrary, each followed his own inclination and his own plan in arranging and ordering his work"<sup>(3)</sup>. "Thus we can no longer distinguish clearly the historical aspects of the person of Jesus, and we no longer possess the sources needed to determine the events of his life precisely"<sup>(4)</sup>.

If, however, we look at it from a purely historical perspective, relying on the reports on which the other historical sources most agree, we find that it began with the appearance of a man called Jesus of Nazareth in the region of Galilee in northern Palestine, during the reign of the Roman emperor Tiberius. He called his people, the Children of Israel, as a messenger sent to them from God, summoning them to righteousness and piety and to the revival of the precepts of the Law revealed to Moses, and announcing the imminent coming of the Kingdom of God, in which there would be no place for the wicked. Some sincere supporters from among his people responded to him and gathered around him. But a violent event ended his life on earth. His work, however, did not end with his end: his followers continued to preach his call. Yet the first century had not passed before we find that his person had taken the foremost place in a new religion, which began to extend and spread, ending in complete separation from, and indeed open hostility to, the religion that Jesus himself had come to preach. This new religion grew strong and its pillars became firmly established until, in the fourth century, it became a strange empire founded in the name of Christ, called Christianity, extending the shadow of its church and its sovereignty with the extent of its possessions and influence on earth.

(2) Al-Masihiyya: Nash'atuha wa Tatawwuruha, p. 26, Charles Guignebert, translated by Abd al-Halim Mahmud, al-Maktaba al-Asriyya,

Beirut.

(3) Ibid., p. 28.

(4) Ibid., p. 29.

{77}------------------------------------------------

Thus, looking at Christianity in its first origin, and in its development and what it ended in, places us before two pictures that differ in everything, except in the link between them, which is the person of Jesus (peace be upon him), a person who has been exploited in the worst way throughout the whole of religious history. The Christ of today is a god; the Christ of yesterday was a man. The Christ of today legislates and lays down laws, permits and forbids, abases and exalts; the Christ of yesterday was a messenger conveying from God and calling to God by His leave, with no more power over his own affairs than human beings have over theirs. The Christ of today is a worshipped creator; the Christ of yesterday was a created servant. The books of yesterday speak of Christ's struggle and sufferings for the sake of his call to God; the books of today speak of the triumphant Christ who must be worshipped and deified with devotion. The books of yesterday speak of the loving Christ prostrate on the ground in humility before his Creator; the books of today speak of the Christ seated on his throne in heaven, with the crown of kingship on his head and in his hand the sceptre of dominion over the kingdom of earth and heaven. If we set about counting the differences between the Christ of yesterday and the Christ of today, we would never finish, and could not, unless we could count the differences between God and man. Those very differences are the content of the whole Christian heritage, or Christian theology (*kalam*); whatever else that heritage contains is only the seepage and secretion of analysis or debate over those differences. It is nothing but the outcome of continuous debate, interpretation, justification and explanation about the relation between the divine nature and the human nature in the person of Jesus. Hence it became the most disputatious and theological of the three religions, revolving around a single problem around which it does not and will not cease to revolve, for its life lies in that revolving; were it to stop, its heart and its breath of life would stop with it. If the scholars of Byzantium had nothing to talk about but the egg and the hen, what would remain of Christianity if talk of the God-man or the man-God, that is, Christ, were removed from it?! Perhaps his name would remain, and some virtues transmitted from him, things which, though true, cannot sustain a milla, nor build a church, nor raise thrones and kingdoms. Here we may rightly say once again: were it not for the Noble Qur'an, nothing would remain of Jesus (peace be upon him) and his message that points to him with certainty, neither milla nor nihla. Everything said about him outside what the Qur'an says is like carded wool; Christian scholars themselves cast doubts on it that almost make it like devoured stalks, or a trace after the substance is gone. What we present of Christianity through its official documents is only a sample to clarify this aspect. From those documents I shall content myself with what serves the purpose, and I shall not be drawn after the Christian historians of religion in everything they have said, nor share their zeal for mockery and demolition, for less than what I present would be enough to achieve the scholarly aim with which we are concerned, without bigotry or partisanship,

{78}------------------------------------------------

namely, to set out the milla as distinct from the nihla in Christianity, as one of the great heavenly religions, a religion faith in whose book and messenger forms one of the pillars of my own faith.

Though this is my aim, it will not be affected, and may perhaps be supported, by a word I find necessary as a preface to speaking about Christianity. I address it, with gentleness and affection, to my friend the young scholar-monk whose hospitality I had the good fortune to enjoy at one of our venerable monasteries in Wadi al-Natrun, and who has given himself and his youth to faith, withdrawing from people in the quiet of his cell in search of the roots and origins of that faith. It will not displease him, just as it will not displease any of our brothers, young scholar-monks like him, who wish to found their faith on truth and piety, goodness and love, nor will the results this chapter arrives at. Know, my friend, that I shall be the first to be glad of the correction of any error in them, provided that the correction rests on sound evidence in substance and in aim. Debate and dialogue for the sake of truth and goodness are praiseworthy, and every true religion and every unbiased mind calls to them, for knowing the truth and acting on it is God's message to people. As for debate for the sake of the self and the victory of an opinion or a belief, even though it has a function, whose educational and social role we might discuss, and which can be justified within the limits of that function, in my judgment it cannot be justified scientifically or morally before truth and goodness, or in the domain of knowledge. I am therefore not fit to be a party to debate of this kind, or an adversary in it. What, in your judgment, would a debate of this kind add beyond what the theologians of Judaism, Christianity or Islam have already added? It would only thicken the darkness with which the theologians have wrapped the divine light in those religions. What I would like to say to you is what Anatole France said: "Your pleasure at what you have seen does not concern me; it is enough for me that what you have seen is the truth. It is not the business of science to arouse pleasure or provoke displeasure; it is a stranger to human emotions"<sup>(5)</sup>. And my aim with you will remain:

> Say: "O People of the Book, come to a word common between us and you: that we worship none but God, and associate nothing with Him, and that none of us take others as lords besides God." But if they turn away, then say: "Bear witness that we are Muslims [those who submit to God]."<sup>(6)</sup>

(5) Introduction, Al-Masihiyya: Nash'atuha wa Tatawwuruha.

(6) Al Imran: 64.

{79}------------------------------------------------

However much our opinions and interpretations may differ, my heart will remain full of love for you and for all your brothers, the learned monks in every monastery of Egypt, which is kept safe by God's leave and then by the faith, love and affection of its people; for my feelings towards you have been shaped by the words of God Most High:

> You will surely find the most hostile of people towards those who believe to be the Jews and those who associate partners with God; and you will surely find the nearest of them in affection to those who believe to be those who say, "We are Christians." That is because among them are priests and monks, and because they are not arrogant. (7)

And now, what is Christianity?

(7) Al-Ma'ida: 81.

{80}------------------------------------------------

**Christianity:** named after the Messiah, Jesus son of Mary (peace be upon him). It is also called *al-Nasraniyya*, after the town of Nazareth in Palestine, the home of Jesus (peace be upon him), the Messenger of God and His Word, sent as a prophet and messenger to the Children of Israel. He was charged with the message when he reached the age of thirty, and his mission lasted three years, three months and three days, according to al-Shahrastani (8).

We saw in the previous chapter how the Jews altered the law of Moses to serve their aims and their desires, and how their materialist thinking took them so far as to deny the foundations of religion, the resurrection and the immortality of the soul, which left its mark on their religious and moral life. Jesus (peace be upon him) was sent to them when desires and lusts had overwhelmed them and corruption had spread through their belief and their morals, until they had taken their own whims as their god. Jesus's call to them was as the Holy Qur'an defined it:

> And the Messiah said: "O Children of Israel, worship God, my Lord and your Lord." (9)

> "God is my Lord and your Lord, so worship Him; this is a straight path." (10)

The Qur'an speaks of Jesus in many verses: of his birth, his mission, and the Jews' hostility to his call and their war against it, especially in Surat Al Imran, Al-Ma'ida and Maryam. Together these form the complete life story of Jesus (peace be upon him): his lineage, his birth, his mission and his end. They also make clear the roots of the doctrinal and theological questions and problems that came to surround the person of Jesus and his mission. I shall present these texts in the order of the suras of the Qur'an, without interfering in the course of that order, and afterwards we will draw the necessary conclusions.

(8) See Al-Milal wa al-Nihal (Religions and Sects) 1/201.

(9) Al-Ma'ida: 72.

(10) Al Imran: 51.

{81}------------------------------------------------

The Qur'an speaks of Jesus (peace be upon him) in twelve of its suras, as follows:

- Surat al-Baqara:

> We gave Moses the Book, and after him We sent messengers in succession, and We gave Jesus son of Mary the clear proofs and strengthened him with the Holy Spirit. (Al-Baqara 2:87)

> Say: "We believe in God and in what has been sent down to us, and in what was sent down to Abraham, Ishmael, Isaac, Jacob and the Tribes, and in what was given to Moses and Jesus, and in what was given to the prophets from their Lord. We make no distinction between any of them, and to Him we submit." (Al-Baqara 2:136)

> Those messengers: We have favoured some of them above others. Among them are those to whom God spoke, and some He raised in rank. And We gave Jesus son of Mary the clear proofs and strengthened him with the Holy Spirit. Had God willed, those who came after them would not have fought one another after the clear proofs had come to them; but they differed, and among them were some who believed and some who disbelieved. Had God willed, they would not have fought one another; but God does whatever He wills. (Al-Baqara 2:253)

{82}------------------------------------------------

> God chose Adam and Noah, the family of Abraham and the family of Imran above all the worlds, descendants one of another; and God is All-Hearing, All-Knowing. When the wife of Imran said: "My Lord, I have vowed to You what is in my womb, dedicated to Your service, so accept it from me; You are the All-Hearing, the All-Knowing." And when she gave birth to her, she said: "My Lord, I have given birth to a female," and God knew best what she had borne, and the male is not like the female, "and I have named her Mary, and I commit her and her offspring to Your protection from Satan the accursed." So her Lord accepted her with a goodly acceptance and made her grow in goodly growth, and placed her in the care of Zechariah. Whenever Zechariah went in to her in the sanctuary, he found provision with her. He said: "O Mary, where does this come to you from?" She said: "It is from God; God provides for whom He wills without reckoning." There and then Zechariah called upon his Lord, saying: "My Lord, grant me from Yourself goodly offspring; You are the Hearer of prayer." Then the angels called to him as he stood praying in the sanctuary: "God gives you glad tidings of John, who will confirm a Word from God, noble, chaste, and a prophet from among the righteous." He said: "My Lord, how shall I have a son when old age has overtaken me and my wife is barren?" He said: "Thus it is: God does what He wills." He said: "My Lord, give me a sign." He said: "Your sign is that you will not speak to people

{83}------------------------------------------------

> for three days except by gestures. And remember your Lord much, and glorify Him in the evening and the morning." And when the angels said: "O Mary, God has chosen you and purified you, and chosen you above the women of all the worlds. O Mary, be devoutly obedient to your Lord, and prostrate and bow with those who bow." That is from the tidings of the unseen which We reveal to you; you were not with them when they cast their pens to decide which of them should have charge of Mary, nor were you with them when they disputed. When the angels said: "O Mary, God gives you glad tidings of a Word from Him, whose name is the Messiah, Jesus son of Mary, honoured in this world and the Hereafter and one of those brought near. He will speak to people in the cradle and in maturity, and he will be one of the righteous." She said: "My Lord, how shall I have a child when no man has touched me?" He said: "Thus it is: God creates what He wills. When He decrees a matter, He only says to it 'Be', and it is. And He will teach him the Book and wisdom, the Torah and the Gospel, and make him a messenger to the Children of Israel: 'I have come to you with a sign from your Lord: I make for you out of clay the likeness of a bird, then I breathe into it and it becomes a bird by God's leave; and I heal the blind from birth and the leper, and I bring the dead to life by God's leave; and I tell you what you eat and what you store up in your houses. Surely in that is a sign for you, if you are believers. And I come confirming the Torah that was before me, and to make lawful for you some of what was forbidden to you;

{84}------------------------------------------------

> and I have come to you with a sign from your Lord, so fear God and obey me. God is my Lord and your Lord, so worship Him; this is a straight path.'" And when Jesus sensed disbelief among them, he said: "Who will be my helpers in the cause of God?" The disciples said: "We are God's helpers. We believe in God; bear witness that we are Muslims (submitted to Him). Our Lord, we believe in what You have sent down and we follow the messenger, so write us down among those who bear witness." And they plotted, and God plotted, and God is the best of plotters. When God said: "O Jesus, I will take you to Myself and raise you up to Me, and purify you of those who disbelieve, and place those who follow you above those who disbelieve until the Day of Resurrection. Then to Me is your return, and I will judge between you in what you used to differ about. As for those who disbelieve, I will punish them with a severe punishment in this world and the Hereafter, and they will have no helpers. And as for those who believe and do righteous deeds, He will pay them their reward in full; and God does not love the wrongdoers." This is what We recite to you of the signs and the wise Reminder. The likeness of Jesus in God's sight is as the likeness of Adam: He created him from dust, then said to him "Be", and he was. The truth is from your Lord, so do not be among the doubters. And whoever disputes with you about him after the knowledge that has come to you, say: "Come, let us call our sons and your sons, our women and your women, ourselves and yourselves, then let us pray earnestly

{85}------------------------------------------------

> and lay God's curse upon those who lie." This is indeed the true account; there is no god but God, and God is the Mighty, the Wise. And if they turn away, God knows well those who work corruption. (Al Imran 3:33-63)

> Say: "We believe in God and in what has been sent down to us, and in what was sent down to Abraham, Ishmael, Isaac, Jacob and the Tribes, and in what was given to Moses and Jesus and the prophets from their Lord. We make no distinction between any of them, and to Him we submit." (Al Imran 3:84)

- Al-Nisa:

> And for their disbelief, and their uttering against Mary a monstrous slander, and their saying: "We killed the Messiah, Jesus son of Mary, the Messenger of God." They did not kill him and they did not crucify him, but it was made to appear so to them. Those who differ about it are in doubt concerning it; they have no knowledge of it, only the following of conjecture, and they certainly did not kill him. Rather, God raised him up to Himself, and God is Mighty, Wise. And there is none of the People of the Book but will surely believe in him before his death, and on the Day of Resurrection he will be a witness against them. (Al-Nisa 4:156-159)

{86}------------------------------------------------

> We have revealed to you as We revealed to Noah and the prophets after him, and We revealed to Abraham, Ishmael, Isaac, Jacob and the Tribes, and to Jesus, Job, Jonah, Aaron and Solomon, and to David We gave the Psalms; and messengers We have told you of before, and messengers We have not told you of; and to Moses God spoke directly; messengers bearing glad tidings and warning, so that people would have no argument against God after the messengers. And God is Mighty, Wise. (Al-Nisa 4:163-165)

> O People of the Book, do not go to extremes in your religion, and say nothing about God but the truth. The Messiah, Jesus son of Mary, was only a messenger of God, and His Word which He conveyed to Mary, and a spirit from Him. So believe in God and His messengers, and do not say "Three". Desist; it is better for you. God is only One God; glory be to Him, far above having a son. To Him belongs whatever is in the heavens and whatever is on the earth, and God suffices as a Guardian. The Messiah would never disdain to be a servant of God, nor would the angels who are brought near. Whoever disdains His worship and is arrogant, He will gather them all to Himself. As for those who believe and do righteous deeds, He will pay them their reward in full and give them more out of His bounty; and as for those who disdain and are arrogant, He will punish them with a painful punishment, and they will find for themselves, besides God, no protector and no helper. (Al-Nisa 4:171-173)

{87}------------------------------------------------

> They have certainly disbelieved who say that God is the Messiah, son of Mary. Say: "Who then could avail anything against God if He willed to destroy the Messiah, son of Mary, and his mother, and everyone on earth altogether?" To God belongs the dominion of the heavens and the earth and all that is between them. He creates what He wills, and God has power over all things. (Al-Ma'ida 5:17)

> And We sent, following in their footsteps, Jesus son of Mary, confirming the Torah that was before him; and We gave him the Gospel, in which is guidance and light, confirming the Torah that was before it, and as guidance and an admonition for the God-fearing. (Al-Ma'ida 5:46)

> They have certainly disbelieved who say that God is the Messiah, son of Mary, whereas the Messiah said: "O Children of Israel, worship God, my Lord and your Lord." Whoever associates partners with God, God has forbidden him Paradise, and his refuge is the Fire, and the wrongdoers will have no helpers. They have certainly disbelieved who say that God is the third of three, when there is no god but One God. If they do not desist from what they say, a painful punishment will surely afflict those of them who disbelieve. Will they not then turn to God in repentance and ask His forgiveness? And God is Forgiving, Merciful. The Messiah, son of Mary, was no more than a messenger; messengers had passed away before him; and his mother was a woman of truth.

{88}------------------------------------------------

> They both used to eat food. See how We make the signs clear to them, then see how they are turned away. Say: "Do you worship, besides God, that which has no power to harm or benefit you? Say: "O People of the Book, do not go to extremes in your religion beyond the truth, and do not follow the desires of a people who went astray before, and led many astray, and strayed from the right way." Those of the Children of Israel who disbelieved were cursed by the tongue of David and of Jesus son of Mary; that was because they disobeyed and used to transgress. (Al-Ma'ida 5:72-78)

> On the day when God will gather the messengers and say: "What response did you receive?" they will say: "We have no knowledge; You alone are the Knower of the unseen." When God will say: "O Jesus son of Mary, remember My favour upon you and upon your mother, when I strengthened you with the Holy Spirit, so that you spoke to people in the cradle and in maturity; and when I taught you the Book and wisdom, the Torah and the Gospel; and when you made out of clay the likeness of a bird by My leave, and breathed into it and it became a bird by My leave; and you healed the blind from birth and the leper by My leave; and when you brought forth the dead by My leave; and when I held back the Children of Israel from you when you came to them with the clear proofs, and those of them who disbelieved said: 'This is nothing but plain sorcery.' And when I inspired the disciples: 'Believe in Me and in My messenger.' They said: 'We believe; bear witness

> that we are Muslims (submitted to You).'" When the disciples said: "O Jesus son of Mary, is your Lord able to send down to us a table spread with food from heaven?" he said: "Fear God, if you are believers." They said: "We wish to eat from it, and to have our hearts reassured, and to know that you have told us the truth, and to be among its witnesses." Jesus son of Mary said: "O God, our Lord, send down to us a table spread with food from heaven, to be a festival for us, for the first of us and the last of us, and a sign from You; and provide for us, for You are the best of providers." God said: "I will send it down to you; but whoever of you disbelieves after that, I will punish him with a punishment with which I have not punished anyone in all the worlds." And when God will say: "O Jesus son of Mary, did you say to people, 'Take me and my mother as two gods besides God'?" he will say: "Glory be to You! It was not for me to say what I had no right to say. Had I said it, You would surely have known it. You know what is in my self, and I do not know what is in Yours; You alone are the Knower of the unseen. I said to them nothing but what You commanded me: 'Worship God, my Lord and your Lord.' And I was a witness over them as long as I remained among them; and when You took me to Yourself, You were the Watcher over them, and You are Witness over all things. If You punish them, they are Your servants; and if You forgive them, You are the Mighty, the Wise." God will say: "This is the day when their truthfulness will benefit the truthful. Theirs are gardens beneath which rivers flow, abiding

> therein forever. God is pleased with them and they are pleased with Him; that is the great triumph." To God belongs the dominion of the heavens and the earth and all that is in them, and He has power over all things. (Al-Ma'ida 5:109-120)

> That was Our argument which We gave Abraham against his people. We raise in rank whom We will; your Lord is Wise, Knowing. And We gave him Isaac and Jacob; each of them We guided. And Zechariah, John, Jesus and Elias; each was among the righteous. (Al-An'am 6:83-85)

May God fight them! How they are turned away! They have taken their rabbis and their monks as lords besides God, and also the Messiah, son of Mary, though they were commanded only to worship One God; there is no god but He. {91}------------------------------------------------

> And mention in the Book Mary, when she withdrew from her family to a place in the east, and placed a screen between herself and them. Then We sent to her Our Spirit, and he appeared to her as a man in perfect form. She said: "I seek refuge in the Most Merciful from you, if you fear God." He said: "I am only a messenger of your Lord, to grant you a pure boy." She said: "How shall I have a boy when no man has touched me and I have not been unchaste?" He said: "Thus it is. Your Lord has said: 'It is easy for Me; and We will make him a sign to people and a mercy from Us.' And it was a matter decreed." So she conceived him and withdrew with him to a distant place. And the pains of labour drove her to the trunk of the palm tree. She said: "Would that I had died before this and been a thing forgotten, utterly forgotten." Then he called to her from beneath her: "Do not grieve; your Lord has placed a stream beneath you. And shake towards you the trunk of the palm tree; it will let fall upon you fresh, ripe dates. So eat and drink and be comforted. And if you see any human being, say: 'I have vowed a fast to the Most Merciful, so I will not speak to any person today.'" Then she brought him to her people, carrying him. They said: "O Mary, you have brought a monstrous thing. O sister of Aaron, your father was not a bad man, nor was your mother unchaste." So she pointed to him. They said: "How can we speak to one who is a child in the cradle?"

{92}------------------------------------------------

> He said: "I am the servant of God. He has given me the Book and made me a prophet, and made me blessed wherever I may be, and enjoined upon me prayer and almsgiving as long as I live, and made me dutiful to my mother, and has not made me arrogant or wretched. Peace be upon me the day I was born, the day I die, and the day I am raised alive." That is Jesus son of Mary: a statement of the truth about which they dispute. It is not for God to take a son; glory be to Him! When He decrees a matter, He only says to it "Be", and it is. "And God is my Lord and your Lord, so worship Him; this is a straight path." (Maryam 19:16-36)

> And when We took from the prophets their covenant, and from you, and from Noah, Abraham, Moses and Jesus son of Mary; We took from them a solemn covenant, that He might question the truthful about their truthfulness; and He has prepared for the disbelievers a painful punishment.
